========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, nice to meet you too. I guess I'm here because... well, it's my job. I'm a translator, mostly technical documents, and for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern in the project files. It's funny, I actually got into translation because my grandfather spoke fluent Polish, and he used to tell me these incredible stories—he had this old silver pocket watch that he brought over from Europe, which I still have on my dresser, actually. It needs to be wound every single day or it just stops dead. 

But anyway, the files. Certain subcontractors and this one colleague of mine, they keep making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. It’s so subtle. My wife says I pay too much attention to details, like when we were repainting the living room last spring, I spent three days just looking at swatches of eggshell white because the natural lighting changes everything in the afternoon... but this is different. They are doing it so it looks like my quality control failed. 

I even took it to IT, and of course, they tell me the audit trail is clean. The IT guy I talked to was eating a tuna sandwich right over his mechanical keyboard, which just seems so unsanitary to me, the crumbs get right under the keys. But regardless of what he says, I know what I'm looking at. I know the record has been arranged to put the blame on me. It's just... exhausting, you know?

Oh, absolutely. There's not a shadow of a doubt in my mind. Like I was saying, I've been watching this pattern for months now. Even with IT telling me the audit trail is clean, I know for a fact the record has been arranged to put the blame on me. They are deliberately making those tiny changes to the translation memory so it looks like my quality control failed.

It’s just amazing the lengths people will go to just to avoid taking responsibility, you know? I ended up having to buy a whole new mailbox. I got one of those heavy-duty steel ones, the kind that lock, because you really can't be too careful with mail theft these days. My wife ordered some specialty coffee from Costa Rica around that time, and we were so worried it would get swiped from the curb before we got home from work. It was a dark roast, really oily beans, makes a fantastic espresso... 

But, wait, I'm sorry, I lost my train of thought. Yes. I am entirely certain my colleague and those subcontractors are altering the files to frame me.

Voices? No, no, I don't hear anything like that. Just the usual office noise, you know? The hum of the server room down the hall, people typing. I actually prefer a quieter environment when I work. I used to have these noise-canceling headphones, the really expensive kind—Sony, I think? I bought them right before a trip to Japan a few years ago. We went for our anniversary, my wife and I. The cherry blossoms were just starting to bloom, and it was incredible. We stayed in this tiny little ryokan in Kyoto, and they served us a traditional breakfast every morning with this grilled fish that was just... I don't even know what kind of fish it was, but it was amazing. I tried to recreate it at home once, but I couldn't get the grill hot enough, and it just stuck to the grates. It took me an hour to scrub the pan clean. I ended up using baking soda and vinegar, which usually works for everything. My grandmother taught me that trick. She used to clean her whole house with just baking soda, vinegar, and a little bit of lemon juice. It smelled so fresh, not like those harsh chemicals they use in the office building. The cleaning crew comes in on Tuesdays and Thursdays, and they use this lemon-scented stuff that just gives me a headache. 

But no, to answer your question, I don't hear any voices or anything. Just the normal sounds.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, nice to meet you too. I guess I'm here because... well, it's my job. I'm a translator, mostly technical documents, and for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern in the project files. It's funny, I actually got into translation because my grandfather spoke fluent Polish, and he used to tell me these incredible stories—he had this old silver pocket watch that he brought over from Europe, which I still have on my dresser, actually. It needs to be wound every single day or it just stops dead. \n\nBut anyway, the files. Certain subcontractors and this one colleague of mine, they keep making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. It’s so subtle. My wife says I pay too much attention to details, like when we were repainting the living room last spring, I spent three days just looking at swatches of eggshell white because the natural lighting changes everything in the afternoon... but this is different. They are doing it so it looks like my quality control failed. \n\nI even took it to IT, and of course, they tell me the audit trail is clean. The IT guy I talked to was eating a tuna sandwich right over his mechanical keyboard, which just seems so unsanitary to me, the crumbs get right under the keys. But regardless of what he says, I know what I'm looking at. I know the record has been arranged to put the blame on me. It's just... exhausting, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe for certain that your colleague and the subcontractors are deliberately altering the files to make you look responsible?\n\nPatient: Oh, absolutely. There's not a shadow of a doubt in my mind. Like I was saying, I've been watching this pattern for months now. Even with IT telling me the audit trail is clean, I know for a fact the record has been arranged to put the blame on me. They are deliberately making those tiny changes to the translation memory so it looks like my quality control failed. \n\nPeople think if they just tweak a timestamp, nobody will notice. It’s like... well, it’s like my neighbor, actually. He backed into my mailbox last winter. He tried to tell me the city snowplow did it, but I found red paint chips right there in the snowbank, and he drives a red Ford pickup. It’s just amazing the lengths people will go to just to avoid taking responsibility, you know? I ended up having to buy a whole new mailbox. I got one of those heavy-duty steel ones, the kind that lock, because you really can't be too careful with mail theft these days. My wife ordered some specialty coffee from Costa Rica around that time, and we were so worried it would get swiped from the curb before we got home from work. It was a dark roast, really oily beans, makes a fantastic espresso... \n\nBut, wait, I'm sorry, I lost my train of thought. Yes. I am entirely certain my colleague and those subcontractors are altering the files to frame me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is there?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, no, I don't hear anything like that. Just the usual office noise, you know? The hum of the server room down the hall, people typing. I actually prefer a quieter environment when I work. I used to have these noise-canceling headphones, the really expensive kind—Sony, I think? I bought them right before a trip to Japan a few years ago. We went for our anniversary, my wife and I. The cherry blossoms were just starting to bloom, and it was incredible. We stayed in this tiny little ryokan in Kyoto, and they served us a traditional breakfast every morning with this grilled fish that was just... I don't even know what kind of fish it was, but it was amazing. I tried to recreate it at home once, but I couldn't get the grill hot enough, and it just stuck to the grates. It took me an hour to scrub the pan clean. I ended up using baking soda and vinegar, which usually works for everything. My grandmother taught me that trick. She used to clean her whole house with just baking soda, vinegar, and a little bit of lemon juice. It smelled so fresh, not like those harsh chemicals they use in the office building. The cleaning crew comes in on Tuesdays and Thursdays, and they use this lemon-scented stuff that just gives me a headache. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, I don't hear any voices or anything. Just the normal sounds.\n\n[Last Question]\nIf someone showed you clear proof that the files were not changed, would you believe it?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Proof? Like what kind of proof? Because IT already tried to show me their version of proof. Like I mentioned earlier, they pulled up the audit trail and told me it was completely clean. But I know what I'm seeing. I know the record has been arranged to put the blame on me, so whatever "proof" they have is just part of it. 

It’s all just digital manipulation anyway, which is so easy to do now. Speaking of digital, my wife and I were actually looking at getting a new digital camera before our next vacation. We usually just use our phones, you know, because the cameras on them are so good now, but the optical zoom on a real camera is just so much better for landscapes. We went to this electronics store over on 4th Street, and the salesman was incredibly pushy about buying the extended warranty. I never buy those. It's a complete racket. It's like when our toaster oven broke last year—the heating element just snapped right in half. I actually tried to fix it myself because I still have this old soldering iron from when I used to build model airplanes as a kid. Mostly balsa wood planes. You have to be so incredibly careful with the model glue, though, or it gets all over your fingers and dries like absolute cement. I remember spending a whole Sunday afternoon once just trying to peel dried glue off my thumb while watching a baseball game... 

But, wait, what was the question? Right, the proof. Yeah, no matter what they try to show me on a screen, I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern in the project files for months. I know that one colleague and those subcontractors keep making those tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. They're doing it so it looks like my quality control failed, and no fake audit trail is going to convince me otherwise.

Read my thoughts? No, I don't think anyone can do that. That sounds like something out of a science fiction movie, you know? Like that one with the people who can bend spoons with their minds. I used to love those movies when I was a teenager. My dad would take me to the theater downtown, the one with the really sticky floors and the neon sign that always had a letter burnt out. It's like how printers are so cheap, but the ink cartridges cost a fortune. I bought a new printer for my home office a few months ago, and I swear the replacement ink cost more than the machine itself. I tried buying those generic refill kits online to save some money, but it ended up leaking all over my desk and ruined a perfectly good stack of legal pads. I always buy the yellow ones, the legal pads, I mean. The white ones just glare too much under the fluorescent lights when I'm working late. 

But no, I don't think anyone is reading my mind. I just know what I see on the screen with the translation files.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Trust people? Generally? Um, no, not really. I mean, I've never been overly suspicious or anything before this whole mess started. I've always had a pretty normal group of friends, good relationships with my coworkers... well, at my last job, anyway. I used to work at this agency downtown, right above a bakery. Oh man, the smell of fresh croissants every morning at 8 AM was just incredible. I used to go down there on my break and get this almond pastry they made. The baker, Francois, he was actually from Lyon. We'd chat a bit in French sometimes, just to keep my skills sharp, you know? It's so easy to lose a language if you aren't speaking it regularly. That's why I started reading novels in Spanish before bed, just to maintain the vocabulary. I tried reading Don Quixote in the original Spanish last year, but honestly, the archaic phrasing was just a bit too much. It's like trying to read Shakespeare when you're just used to reading the daily newspaper. I actually still get the physical newspaper delivered on Sundays. There's just something about the feeling of the paper, the ink on your fingers... my wife hates it, says it clutters up the recycling bin, but I like doing the crossword puzzle with a real pen. I only use blue ink, never black. Black ink just feels so... severe, I guess? 

But yeah, trusting people. I've never had a problem with it before. It's just this specific situation with the files, you know? It's right there in front of me.

I'm a translator, mostly technical documents, and for months now I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern in the project files. It's funny, I actually got into translation because my grandfather spoke fluent Polish, and he used to tell me these incredible stories—he had this old silver pocket watch that he brought over from Europe, which I still have on my dresser, actually. It needs to be wound every single day or it just stops dead. \n\nBut anyway, the files. Certain subcontractors and this one colleague of mine, they keep making these tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. It’s so subtle. My wife says I pay too much attention to details, like when we were repainting the living room last spring, I spent three days just looking at swatches of eggshell white because the natural lighting changes everything in the afternoon... but this is different. They are doing it so it looks like my quality control failed. \n\nI even took it to IT, and of course, they tell me the audit trail is clean. The IT guy I talked to was eating a tuna sandwich right over his mechanical keyboard, which just seems so unsanitary to me, the crumbs get right under the keys. But regardless of what he says, I know what I'm looking at. I know the record has been arranged to put the blame on me. It's just... exhausting, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe for certain that your colleague and the subcontractors are deliberately altering the files to make you look responsible?\n\nPatient: Oh, absolutely. There's not a shadow of a doubt in my mind. Like I was saying, I've been watching this pattern for months now. Even with IT telling me the audit trail is clean, I know for a fact the record has been arranged to put the blame on me. They are deliberately making those tiny changes to the translation memory so it looks like my quality control failed. \n\nPeople think if they just tweak a timestamp, nobody will notice. It’s like... well, it’s like my neighbor, actually. He backed into my mailbox last winter. He tried to tell me the city snowplow did it, but I found red paint chips right there in the snowbank, and he drives a red Ford pickup. It’s just amazing the lengths people will go to just to avoid taking responsibility, you know? I ended up having to buy a whole new mailbox. I got one of those heavy-duty steel ones, the kind that lock, because you really can't be too careful with mail theft these days. My wife ordered some specialty coffee from Costa Rica around that time, and we were so worried it would get swiped from the curb before we got home from work. It was a dark roast, really oily beans, makes a fantastic espresso... \n\nBut, wait, I'm sorry, I lost my train of thought. Yes. I am entirely certain my colleague and those subcontractors are altering the files to frame me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is there?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, no, I don't hear anything like that. Just the usual office noise, you know? The hum of the server room down the hall, people typing. I actually prefer a quieter environment when I work. I used to have these noise-canceling headphones, the really expensive kind—Sony, I think? I bought them right before a trip to Japan a few years ago. We went for our anniversary, my wife and I. The cherry blossoms were just starting to bloom, and it was incredible. We stayed in this tiny little ryokan in Kyoto, and they served us a traditional breakfast every morning with this grilled fish that was just... I don't even know what kind of fish it was, but it was amazing. I tried to recreate it at home once, but I couldn't get the grill hot enough, and it just stuck to the grates. It took me an hour to scrub the pan clean. I ended up using baking soda and vinegar, which usually works for everything. My grandmother taught me that trick. She used to clean her whole house with just baking soda, vinegar, and a little bit of lemon juice. It smelled so fresh, not like those harsh chemicals they use in the office building. The cleaning crew comes in on Tuesdays and Thursdays, and they use this lemon-scented stuff that just gives me a headache. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, I don't hear any voices or anything. Just the normal sounds.\n\nDoctor: If someone showed you clear proof that the files were not changed, would you believe it?\n\nPatient: Proof? Like what kind of proof? Because IT already tried to show me their version of proof. Like I mentioned earlier, they pulled up the audit trail and told me it was completely clean. But I know what I'm seeing. I know the record has been arranged to put the blame on me, so whatever \"proof\" they have is just part of it. \n\nIt’s all just digital manipulation anyway, which is so easy to do now. Speaking of digital, my wife and I were actually looking at getting a new digital camera before our next vacation. We usually just use our phones, you know, because the cameras on them are so good now, but the optical zoom on a real camera is just so much better for landscapes. We went to this electronics store over on 4th Street, and the salesman was incredibly pushy about buying the extended warranty. I never buy those. It's a complete racket. It's like when our toaster oven broke last year—the heating element just snapped right in half. I actually tried to fix it myself because I still have this old soldering iron from when I used to build model airplanes as a kid. Mostly balsa wood planes. You have to be so incredibly careful with the model glue, though, or it gets all over your fingers and dries like absolute cement. I remember spending a whole Sunday afternoon once just trying to peel dried glue off my thumb while watching a baseball game... \n\nBut, wait, what was the question? Right, the proof. Yeah, no matter what they try to show me on a screen, I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern in the project files for months. I know that one colleague and those subcontractors keep making those tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. They're doing it so it looks like my quality control failed, and no fake audit trail is going to convince me otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe other people can read your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Read my thoughts? No, I don't think anyone can do that. That sounds like something out of a science fiction movie, you know? Like that one with the people who can bend spoons with their minds. I used to love those movies when I was a teenager. My dad would take me to the theater downtown, the one with the really sticky floors and the neon sign that always had a letter burnt out. We'd get a huge bucket of popcorn, and he'd always complain about how much extra butter they put on it, but then he'd eat most of it anyway. He had this whole theory about movie theater popcorn, actually, that they purposely make it super salty so you have to buy the giant sodas. It makes sense from a business perspective, I guess. It's like how printers are so cheap, but the ink cartridges cost a fortune. I bought a new printer for my home office a few months ago, and I swear the replacement ink cost more than the machine itself. I tried buying those generic refill kits online to save some money, but it ended up leaking all over my desk and ruined a perfectly good stack of legal pads. I always buy the yellow ones, the legal pads, I mean. The white ones just glare too much under the fluorescent lights when I'm working late. \n\nBut no, I don't think anyone is reading my mind. I just know what I see on the screen with the translation files.\n\nDoctor: Before this problem at work, have you generally found it hard to trust other people?\n\nPatient: Trust people? Generally? Um, no, not really. I mean, I've never been overly suspicious or anything before this whole mess started. I've always had a pretty normal group of friends, good relationships with my coworkers... well, at my last job, anyway. I used to work at this agency downtown, right above a bakery. Oh man, the smell of fresh croissants every morning at 8 AM was just incredible. I used to go down there on my break and get this almond pastry they made. The baker, Francois, he was actually from Lyon. We'd chat a bit in French sometimes, just to keep my skills sharp, you know? It's so easy to lose a language if you aren't speaking it regularly. That's why I started reading novels in Spanish before bed, just to maintain the vocabulary. I tried reading Don Quixote in the original Spanish last year, but honestly, the archaic phrasing was just a bit too much. It's like trying to read Shakespeare when you're just used to reading the daily newspaper. I actually still get the physical newspaper delivered on Sundays. There's just something about the feeling of the paper, the ink on your fingers... my wife hates it, says it clutters up the recycling bin, but I like doing the crossword puzzle with a real pen. I only use blue ink, never black. Black ink just feels so... severe, I guess? \n\nBut yeah, trusting people. I've never had a problem with it before. It's just this specific situation with the files, you know? It's right there in front of me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. More than a month? Oh, absolutely. It's been going on for about 11 months now. It started shortly after I was put in charge of the new localization project. It was a massive project, and honestly, the reference materials they provided were just a complete mess from day one. 

Speaking of messes, you should have seen my backyard right around that same time. My neighbor, Gary, came over while I was buffing the hood and started talking my ear off about his golf swing. He's always trying to fix his slice, but he refuses to just pay for a lesson... 

Anyway, sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the timeline. Yeah, 11 months. Ever since I took over that localization project, it's been a constant thing with them altering the files.

She used to clean her whole house with just baking soda, vinegar, and a little bit of lemon juice. It smelled so fresh, not like those harsh chemicals they use in the office building. The cleaning crew comes in on Tuesdays and Thursdays, and they use this lemon-scented stuff that just gives me a headache. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, I don't hear any voices or anything. Just the normal sounds.\n\nDoctor: If someone showed you clear proof that the files were not changed, would you believe it?\n\nPatient: Proof? Like what kind of proof? Because IT already tried to show me their version of proof. Like I mentioned earlier, they pulled up the audit trail and told me it was completely clean. But I know what I'm seeing. I know the record has been arranged to put the blame on me, so whatever \"proof\" they have is just part of it. \n\nIt’s all just digital manipulation anyway, which is so easy to do now. Speaking of digital, my wife and I were actually looking at getting a new digital camera before our next vacation. We usually just use our phones, you know, because the cameras on them are so good now, but the optical zoom on a real camera is just so much better for landscapes. We went to this electronics store over on 4th Street, and the salesman was incredibly pushy about buying the extended warranty. I never buy those. It's a complete racket. It's like when our toaster oven broke last year—the heating element just snapped right in half. I actually tried to fix it myself because I still have this old soldering iron from when I used to build model airplanes as a kid. Mostly balsa wood planes. You have to be so incredibly careful with the model glue, though, or it gets all over your fingers and dries like absolute cement. I remember spending a whole Sunday afternoon once just trying to peel dried glue off my thumb while watching a baseball game... \n\nBut, wait, what was the question? Right, the proof. Yeah, no matter what they try to show me on a screen, I’ve been seeing the exact same pattern in the project files for months. I know that one colleague and those subcontractors keep making those tiny changes to the translation memory and the timestamps. No, I don't think anyone can do that. That sounds like something out of a science fiction movie, you know? Like that one with the people who can bend spoons with their minds. I used to love those movies when I was a teenager. My dad would take me to the theater downtown, the one with the really sticky floors and the neon sign that always had a letter burnt out. We'd get a huge bucket of popcorn, and he'd always complain about how much extra butter they put on it, but then he'd eat most of it anyway. He had this whole theory about movie theater popcorn, actually, that they purposely make it super salty so you have to buy the giant sodas. It makes sense from a business perspective, I guess. It's like how printers are so cheap, but the ink cartridges cost a fortune. I bought a new printer for my home office a few months ago, and I swear the replacement ink cost more than the machine itself. I tried buying those generic refill kits online to save some money, but it ended up leaking all over my desk and ruined a perfectly good stack of legal pads. I always buy the yellow ones, the legal pads, I mean. The white ones just glare too much under the fluorescent lights when I'm working late. \n\nBut no, I don't think anyone is reading my mind. I just know what I see on the screen with the translation files.\n\nDoctor: Before this problem at work, have you generally found it hard to trust other people?\n\nPatient: Trust people? Generally? Um, no, not really. I mean, I've never been overly suspicious or anything before this whole mess started. That's why I started reading novels in Spanish before bed, just to maintain the vocabulary. I tried reading Don Quixote in the original Spanish last year, but honestly, the archaic phrasing was just a bit too much. It's like trying to read Shakespeare when you're just used to reading the daily newspaper. I actually still get the physical newspaper delivered on Sundays. I only use blue ink, never black. Black ink just feels so... severe, I guess? \n\nBut yeah, trusting people. I've never had a problem with it before. Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick). It’s like... well, it’s like my neighbor, actually. He backed into my mailbox last winter. He tried to tell me the city snowplow did it, but I found red paint chips right there in the snowbank, and he drives a red Ford pickup. It’s just amazing the lengths people will go to just to avoid taking responsibility, you know? I ended up having to buy a whole new mailbox. I got one of those heavy-duty steel ones, the kind that lock, because you really can't be too careful with mail theft these days. My wife ordered some specialty coffee from Costa Rica around that time, and we were so worried it would get swiped from the curb before we got home from work. It was a dark roast, really oily beans, makes a fantastic espresso... \n\nBut, wait, I'm sorry, I lost my train of thought. Yes. I am entirely certain my colleague and those subcontractors are altering the files to frame me.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Err toward UNKNOWN (omit) rather than guessing.\n\nReply ONLY with valid JSON (no markdown, no extra text):\n{\n  \"confirmed\": [\"S001\", ...],\n  \"denied\": [\"S025\", ...],\n  \"reasoning\": {\n    \"S001\": \"one-sentence justification\",\n    ...\n  }\n}"
  }
]

(Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others. Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nVoices? No, no, I don't hear anything like that. Just the usual office noise, you know? The hum of the server room down the hall, people typing. I actually prefer a quieter environment when I work. I used to have these noise-canceling headphones, the really expensive kind—Sony, I think? I bought them right before a trip to Japan a few years ago. We went for our anniversary, my wife and I. The cherry blossoms were just starting to bloom, and it was incredible. We stayed in this tiny little ryokan in Kyoto, and they served us a traditional breakfast every morning with this grilled fish that was just... I don't even know what kind of fish it was, but it was amazing. I tried to recreate it at home once, but I couldn't get the grill hot enough, and it just stuck to the grates. It took me an hour to scrub the pan clean. I ended up using baking soda and vinegar, which usually works for everything. My grandmother taught me that trick. She used to clean her whole house with just baking soda, vinegar, and a little bit of lemon juice. It smelled so fresh, not like those harsh chemicals they use in the office building. The cleaning crew comes in on Tuesdays and Thursdays, and they use this lemon-scented stuff that just gives me a headache. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, I don't hear any voices or anything. Just the normal sounds.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. It's like when our toaster oven broke last year—the heating element just snapped right in half. Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nRead my thoughts? No, I don't think anyone can do that. That sounds like something out of a science fiction movie, you know? Like that one with the people who can bend spoons with their minds. I used to love those movies when I was a teenager. My dad would take me to the theater downtown, the one with the really sticky floors and the neon sign that always had a letter burnt out. We'd get a huge bucket of popcorn, and he'd always complain about how much extra butter they put on it, but then he'd eat most of it anyway. He had this whole theory about movie theater popcorn, actually, that they purposely make it super salty so you have to buy the giant sodas. It makes sense from a business perspective, I guess. It's like how printers are so cheap, but the ink cartridges cost a fortune. I bought a new printer for my home office a few months ago, and I swear the replacement ink cost more than the machine itself. I tried buying those generic refill kits online to save some money, but it ended up leaking all over my desk and ruined a perfectly good stack of legal pads. I always buy the yellow ones, the legal pads, I mean. The white ones just glare too much under the fluorescent lights when I'm working late. \n\nBut no, I don't think anyone is reading my mind. I just know what I see on the screen with the translation files.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate.
